| D-QA-06 | Shared quality gate and profile "Bothy way" as files, with an alignment tool for local and cloud. | Same conditions in both places. | [ADR 0040](adr/0040-sonarqube-local-and-cloud-with-the-same-profile-and-gate.md), [sonarqube-local.md](quality/sonarqube-local.md) |
| D-QA-07 | SonarScanner as an npm package. | The same scanner locally and in CI, without Java and Docker. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools) |
| D-QA-08 | Release binaries of the tools with a checksum lock per platform. | Protection against rewritten release assets. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#toolchain) |
| D-QA-09 | Trivy held at a version proven safe, only for images and IaC; OSV-Scanner checks dependencies. | Supply chain incident at Trivy; newer versions not proven safe. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#trivy-keep-the-version) |
| D-QA-10 | Link check as a gate only offline. | Third-party servers make gates non-reproducible. | [R-QA-09](spec/23-quality.md#10-rulings) |
| D-QA-11 | Docs site pinned, GitHub-style anchors, build without `--quiet`. | `--quiet` suppresses warnings in such a way that `--strict` no longer fails. | [ADR 0045](adr/0045-documentation-site-generator.md) |
| D-QA-12 | No `.sonarcloud.properties`; analysis only through CI. | Both ways at the same time interfere with each other. | [R-QA-10](spec/23-quality.md#10-rulings) |
| D-QA-13 | The deny-list scan checks all commits of `HEAD` without a remote; identities only against the private list; the output masks matches. | The first push is the most dangerous one; CI logs must not reveal the list. | [R-QA-08](spec/23-quality.md#10-rulings), [ADR 0042](adr/0042-deny-list-scanner-with-the-list-from-the-ops-repo.md) |
| D-QA-14 | DCO check from the first remote on. | New contributions need proof of origin. | [R-QA-07](spec/23-quality.md#10-rulings) |
| D-QA-15 | gitleaks over all commits reachable from `HEAD`, over all refs before switching to public; narrowly scoped exceptions for example values; own rules with tests. | Example values need a machine-readable marking. | [gates.md](quality/gates.md#list) |
| D-QA-16 | Explicit switches for contributions from forks, visible in the output. | Forks have neither the private list nor a SonarQube server. | [R-QA-02](spec/23-quality.md#10-rulings) |
| D-QA-17 | Server images are tagged only after the scan of the digest. | A vulnerable image never gets a version tag. | [release.yml](../.github/workflows/release.yml), [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#github-actions) |
| D-QA-18 | Attestations only in the public state. | On the repository's plan, attestations exist only when it is public; keyless signatures would expose a private repo. | [R-RL-04](spec/18-release.md#13-decisions-of-this-chapter), [ADR 0062](adr/0062-release-signatures-while-the-repo-is-private.md) |
| D-QA-19 | Known findings in development tools that are not shipped are ignored with a reason and an expiry date. | Not exploitable in deployment; the expiry date forces a new check. | [osv-scanner.toml](../tools/node/osv-scanner.toml) |
| D-QA-20 | mermaid-cli pinned to major version 11. | All diagrams were checked with it; a change comes as a Renovate PR. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools) |
| D-QA-21 | The ADR index is generated and checked in `make docs`. | The index never goes stale unnoticed. | [ADR 0001](adr/0001-adrs-in-madr-format-decisions-as-ruling-log.md), [adr/README.md](adr/README.md) |
| D-QA-22 | Component contract: the root Makefile finds components by their manifests; release components and tags are in one place. | One place for the component list; missing parts fail instead of being skipped silently. | [23 3](spec/23-quality.md#3-component-contract), [components.py](../tools/ci/components.py) |
| D-QA-23 | CodeRabbit with data minimization; the CLI only with an explicit switch. | The CLI sends the diff to the service. | [push-discipline.md](quality/push-discipline.md#coderabbit-cli-and-data-outflow) |
| D-QA-24 | List of allowed licenses; LGPL and GPL in statically linked parts only with an ADR. | Compatible with Apache-2.0. | [licenses.md](quality/licenses.md#allowed-licenses) |
| D-QA-25 | Guard tests keep the numbers of the table in 23 and the versions in `toolchain.md` equal to the configuration and the pins. | No fixed numbers in docs that no test holds. | [test_config_values.py](../tools/docs/tests/test_config_values.py), [test_toolchain_doc.py](../tools/docs/tests/test_toolchain_doc.py) |

## 6. Core contract

### 6.1 Decisions of the core report

The decisions of the core contract beyond the basis are recorded as R-IDs in chapters
00, 03, 04 and 05. Later numbers were added with the integration, the gaps and the addendum.

| Chapter | Decisions | Location |
|---|---|---|
| 03 Data model | `R-DM-01` ff. | [03 8](spec/03-data-model.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| 04 Sync protocol | `R-SY-01` ff. | [04 17](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#17-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| 05 Actions | `R-AZ-01` ff. | [05 8](spec/05-actions.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter) |

### 6.2 Open items of the core report

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-KB-01 | `auth.dpop_nonce_ttl` is defined in 08. | One name, one chapter (D-L4). | [08 18](spec/08-identity.md#18-configuration-values-of-this-chapter) |
| D-KB-02 | The periods of the retention classes are defined in 20; 03 only names the classes. | Periods are a privacy decision. | [20 4.1](spec/20-privacy.md#41-periods-per-retention-class), [03 3](spec/03-data-model.md#3-retention-classes) |
| D-KB-03 | Language selection and ranking of the search vectors are defined in 14. | Search owns this subject. | [14 3](spec/14-search.md#3-languages-and-search-vectors) |
| D-KB-04 | A central configuration reference is generated from the chapter tables. | Same as D-L4. | [configuration.md 4](operations/configuration.md#4-named-values-of-all-chapters) |
| D-KB-05 | Device keys only with `ES256` (and `EdDSA` for server keys), until a Windows spike proves whether Windows Hello delivers `ES256`. | Not proven; another algorithm family without a need would be attack surface. | [03 `device_keys`](spec/03-data-model.md#device_keys), D-K-C12 |
| D-KB-06 | ADRs 0047 to 0065 from the list of the lead exist. | During the integration they were only referenced. | [ADR index](adr/README.md) |

### 6.3 Requests from the teams to the core contract

Every request from a team appears here exactly once with decision, reason and location. "adopted"
means: changed in the core contract and carried over into the dependent documents. "same as A7"
refers to the row `D-K-A7`.

| D-K-A2 | Name the River tables as technical tables | adopted | Complete list of the tables, owner `platform/jobs` | [03 4.13](spec/03-data-model.md#413-technical-tables); [02 4.2](spec/02-architecture.md#42-module-list) |
| D-K-A3 | Fencing column on `agent_consumer_cursors` | adopted | `4015` must be enforceable across several api nodes | [03](spec/03-data-model.md) `holder_session_id` with a conditional `UPDATE`; [04 15.1](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#151-connection-and-cursor) |
| D-K-A4 | Catch-up filter for new channels in visible spaces | adopted | Otherwise channels that are created after the cache is built are missing | [04 7.2](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#72-flow) (`$visible_spaces`, `$fully_visible_spaces`, `$instance_wide`), [04 8.1](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#81-basic-rule) (structure events in log order) |

| D-K-A9 | Action for health endpoints | rejected | Ruling L2: health on the internal listener outside the API | [05](spec/05-actions.md) [R-AZ-22](spec/05-actions.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter), [06](spec/06-api.md) [R-API-16](spec/06-api.md#19-decisions-of-this-chapter), [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) without `/health/*` |
| D-K-A10 | Modules `gateway`, `search`, `stats` in 03 | adopted | Complete module list (L3) | [03](spec/03-data-model.md) module table (without tables), [02 4.2](spec/02-architecture.md#42-module-list) |

| D-K-X17 | Second device key in the API | `DeviceRegistration.step_up_key`, `Device.step_up_key_thumbprint` (L10) | [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) |
| D-K-X18 | Gitleaks rule for product tokens | `bothy-api-token` with a test (follows from H1) | [.gitleaks.toml](../.gitleaks.toml), [test_gitleaks_rules.py](../tools/ci/tests/test_gitleaks_rules.py) |

## 7. Addendum 2026-10-10: terminal, visualization, showcase

After the teams had finished, the owner gave an addendum: the product is a
"command center for AI agents in chat form" ([R-PR-13](spec/01-product.md#13-decisions-of-this-chapter)) with the terminal as a core function from M2,
native visualizations and a Grafana panel in the same design language, and everything visible
must meet the showcase standard. The lead decided the design with rulings T1 to T11; before that,
a fresh reviewer read it against the specification and the owner's wording. Where T1 to T11
contradict the first draft, they apply. The writers then wrote chapters 24 and 25, the
ADRs 0068 to 0070, the core contract and the plan; their own rulings follow in 7.2
to 7.5.

### 7.1 Rulings of the lead (T1 to T11)

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-T1 | Guard rule SR10: a person who is not an owner gets a terminal only on a host assigned to them (`hosts.assigned_user_id`) or on their own ready dev environment (from V2). Grants to others can be created; the preview says that they have no effect. On the server host, the host service refuses sessions of non-owners. | 21 and [ADR 0046](adr/0046-multi-user-dev-environment-only-with-isolation.md) reject the OS user as the sole boundary between people; a shared home directory shares LLM logins. | [07 SR10](spec/07-permissions.md#sr10-terminal-only-on-own-host), [24 8.2](spec/24-terminal.md#82-protection-rules), [R-TM-05](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T2 | I6 with owner exception: on a host with `llm_credentials = personal` only `credential_holder_id`, not even another owner; there only an individual grant to the holder, no group grant. [ADR 0068](adr/0068-terminal-through-a-host-service-with-noise-stream-and-invariant-i6.md) distinguishes this from [ADR 0024](adr/0024-backend-policy-personal-organization-with-invariant.md) and D-L22. In addition, with `none` or `organization` the host service does not start when it detects a subscription login of the coding agent; the profile `claude` builds its environment like the runner. | The pattern of I1 would make personal hosts impossible with two owners; a group synchronization cannot be refused. A terminal is access to someone else's host, not an inherited level. | [07 SR11](spec/07-permissions.md#sr11-terminal-and-llm-login-i6), [24 7.3](spec/24-terminal.md#73-invariant-i6), [24 7.4](spec/24-terminal.md#74-enforcement-on-the-host), [ADR 0068](adr/0068-terminal-through-a-host-service-with-noise-stream-and-invariant-i6.md) |
| D-T3 | Protection goal of the end-to-end encryption: relay, proxy, logs, database dump, an api process that reads along. Not: a malicious instance operator running the server. Every device pins the host's key on the first opening (fingerprint visible), with a hard warning on a change; a re-pairing creates an audit entry and an owner notification. | Noise NK only proves the key that the server distributes; 19 assumes the server is trustworthy. | [24 5.1](spec/24-terminal.md#51-protection-goal), [R-TM-03](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-SE-12](spec/19-security.md#15-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T4 | A new ADR partly supersedes [ADR 0028](adr/0028-web-target-only-for-the-admin-and-statistics-console.md): terminal in the web console. `terminal.open` there only with WebAuthn `userVerification=required`, no fallback path; hardening (Trusted Types, strict CSP) as a control in 19. | The owner requires the terminal in the console and passkey confirmation; otherwise 08 allows step-up without a passkey in the console. | [ADR 0070](adr/0070-terminal-in-the-web-console.md), [08 6.3](spec/08-identity.md#63-fresh-step-up-for-the-terminal), [19 11a](spec/19-security.md#11a-controls-for-the-terminal) |
| D-T5 | In this version, "Claude Code sessions" means: interactive `claude` in the terminal, separate from the runner. Taking over a thread in the terminal only becomes possible once the runner and the host service run as the same user in a V2 environment (V3). | The runner's session lock; the runner user holds secrets of the instance operator. | [24 11.3](spec/24-terminal.md#113-claude-code-sessions) |
| D-T6 | Multiple nodes: the server instructs the host service over the control connection to open one data WebSocket per session with the session ID in the path; the proxy routes both paths of the same session to the same node by hash. | 02 forbids a broker; the event log is not suited for byte streams. | [24 9](spec/24-terminal.md#9-multiple-nodes), [R-TM-09](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-OP-19](spec/16-operations.md#15-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T7 | The live statistics measure on their own: the server counts its own SLI values with bounds from the Sloth files, minute aggregates in a separate table with its own retention; `stats.live` and the timeline read from it; the traffic light is called "own measurement". | [R-OB-08](spec/17-observability.md#12-decisions-of-this-chapter) and 17 forbid reading from the telemetry. | [25 3](spec/25-visualization.md#3-own-measurement), [R-OB-10](spec/17-observability.md#12-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-DM-38](spec/03-data-model.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T8 | The product shows a backend view: billing mode, configured and reported mode, switches, costs and the rate-limit state reported by the adapter. Data about individual subscription seats or credentials does not belong in the product. | The product knows no credentials (I5). | [25 6.2](spec/25-visualization.md#62-backend-view), [R-VZ-08](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter), [ADR 0069](adr/0069-visualization-on-two-levels-without-iframes-in-apps.md) |
| D-T9 | Handshake: the terminal session token is valid exactly once (the host service remembers its ID until expiry); session ID and protocol version are in the Noise prologue; the PTY starts only after the first valid transport message of the client. | Otherwise the relay can replay the first packet and create PTYs that nobody operates. | [24 5.3](spec/24-terminal.md#53-terminal-session-token), [R-TM-02](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T10 | Keys of the host service as a file with permissions `0600` in the state directory, on Windows with DPAPI; keychain only where available. The login of the coding agent for service users via a long-lived token; the build verifies this path ([assumed] until then). | The keychain of a service user without a login session is not unlocked. | [24 4.2](spec/24-terminal.md#42-storage-on-the-host) |
| D-T11 | The Grafana plugin stays unsigned until V1 and is provisioned locally. Signing needs a Grafana Cloud account; that is an owner question in `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` (default: unsigned locally, account at publication). | The owner decides on an external account. | [25 8.3](spec/25-visualization.md#83-distribution), [R-VZ-11](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |

### 7.2 Findings of the reviewers, fixed by the writers

The writers assigned these IDs themselves; they stay.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-T12 | Host service with two OS users: the service user holds keys, connections and Noise; the PTYs run as the session user (`host_service.session_user`) without read permission on the state directory, connected over a local channel with verification of the peer. New doctor check HD8. Current state: a separate session user per person with its own PTY helper (`host_service.session_users`), D-W-57. | Otherwise every person with a terminal can copy the keys, impersonate the host from elsewhere and read along in later sessions. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host), [24 4.2](spec/24-terminal.md#42-storage-on-the-host), [16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service), [R-TM-12](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T13 | A second live control connection with the same keys disables the host (`duplicate_connection`, audit, notification to the owners, re-enabling only by re-pairing); if the older one does not answer within `terminal.host_duplicate_probe_timeout`, it is replaced. | Silent displacement would give an attacker with copied keys every later session; the probe avoids false alarms after network changes. | [24 3.4](spec/24-terminal.md#34-second-control-connection), [R-TM-13](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T14 | HD2 rejects service users in groups with admin permissions, including `docker`, `lxd`, `libvirt`, `disk`, plus `host_service.extra_forbidden_groups`; the Docker socket must not be writable. | According to the vendor documentation these groups give root-level permissions; the owner requires a user without admin permissions. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host) |
| D-T15 | HD6 (subscription login) runs in the account of the session user before every PTY and every `host_service.credential_check_interval`; a hit ends all sessions of the host with `host_policy_violation`. Current state: HD6 runs in every session user and since D-W-57 is only an additional check; the load-bearing measure is the separate session user per person. | A subscription login can come into existence during a session and would otherwise be used by all later sessions (I6). | [24 7.4](spec/24-terminal.md#74-enforcement-on-the-host), [R-TM-14](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T16 | Every session in its own process group, on Linux in its own cgroup, on Windows in a Job Object with `KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`; the end terminates the whole tree. Long-running processes remain an honestly documented limit. | A revocation should also end background processes. | [24 6.5](spec/24-terminal.md#65-end-of-a-session-on-the-host), [R-TM-15](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T17 | Quick reattach without step-up never applies in the web console; pinning in the console does not protect against a malicious server (there only `require_local_confirm`). | The console has no device key, and the server delivers its code itself. | [24 6.2](spec/24-terminal.md#62-detach-and-reattach), [24 5.5](spec/24-terminal.md#55-key-pinning-on-the-device), [ADR 0070](adr/0070-terminal-in-the-web-console.md) |
| D-T18 | An expired or consumed token closes with `4101`, every other token or handshake error with `4104`. | Cases without an attack must not be reported as tampering; AsyncAPI and the chapter contradicted each other. | [24 5.3](spec/24-terminal.md#53-terminal-session-token), [R-TM-16](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T19 | Noise test vectors from two independent implementations (cacophony, noise-c), with commit hash, in the test directory of the host service. | The Noise specification itself contains no test vectors; the conformance test needs a documented source. | [24 5.2](spec/24-terminal.md#52-protocol), [ADR 0068](adr/0068-terminal-through-a-host-service-with-noise-stream-and-invariant-i6.md) |
| D-T20 | The mapping from SLO to metric of the own measurement is a Sloth label `bothy_sli_metric` per SLO; `slos.md` shows it as a column; PLAN M6-11 sets the labels. | One source with the SLOs instead of a second list. | [25 3.3](spec/25-visualization.md#33-bounds-from-the-sloth-files), [slos.md 2](observability/slos.md#2-slos), [PLAN M6-11](PLAN.md#block-visualization) |
| D-T21 | Live topology, backend view, delivery and timeline also in the mobile app; only TV mode has no mobile version. | The owner requires the views for the console and the apps, "nothing gets cut down". | [25 1](spec/25-visualization.md#1-purpose-and-limits), [01 3](spec/01-product.md#3-feature-list), [PLAN M6-16](PLAN.md#block-visualization) |
| D-T22 | Golden matrix of `bothy_viz` with the densities `comfortable`, `compact` and `touch`. | An untested density would be a gap. | [25 7.3](spec/25-visualization.md#73-tests), [PLAN M6-12](PLAN.md#block-visualization) |
| D-T23 | New jobs `stats.retention` and `terminal.sweep` in the job catalogue. | Sessions of a failed node need a fallback; the names were missing from the catalogue. | [02 12.3](spec/02-architecture.md#123-job-catalogue), [24 6.3](spec/24-terminal.md#63-limits) |
| D-T24 | M7-28 includes a changelog summary per release with a check, architecture graphics readable in light and dark mode and a one-minute test with a fresh reviewer. | The owner names architecture graphics, changelog and "understand in one minute". | [PLAN M7-28](PLAN.md#13-m7-operations-privacy-release-and-showcase) |

### 7.3 Terminal

| ID | Writer ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-T25 | N-T-01 | A host is not an entry in `principals` but a separate principal kind `host` only in the principal context, authenticated by an Ed25519 challenge. | A host needs neither a handle nor a grant subject; a new kind in `principals` would have affected groups, grants and mentions. | [07 3](spec/07-permissions.md#3-principal-context), [05 4.14](spec/05-actions.md#414-hosts-and-terminal), [24 4.1](spec/24-terminal.md#41-two-key-pairs-per-host) |
| D-T26 | N-T-02 | Additional actions `host.pair`, `terminal.attach`, `terminal.stream` and the system action `terminal.terminate`; `host.connect` covers the control and data connection. | Every route and every op needs exactly one action; server-side terminations name a known action. | [05 4.14](spec/05-actions.md#414-hosts-and-terminal), [02 4.6](spec/02-architecture.md#46-actions-per-module) |
| D-T27 | N-T-03 | `grants.level` is empty exactly when `target_kind = 'host'`; there `capabilities = {terminal}`; in the API `level` is therefore no longer required. | A target without levels in the same grant table; a placeholder level would be misleading. | [03 `grants`](spec/03-data-model.md#grants), [R-DM-35](spec/03-data-model.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T28 | N-T-04 | Step-up `fresh`: younger than `terminal.step_up_max_age`, only passkey or step-up device key, consumed via `auth_sessions.step_up_consumed_at`. | Owner requirement "renewed passkey confirmation on opening", taken literally per opening. | [05 2.4](spec/05-actions.md#24-columns-owner-step-up-audit-permissions), [08 6.3](spec/08-identity.md#63-fresh-step-up-for-the-terminal), [R-DM-37](spec/03-data-model.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T29 | N-T-05 | Quick reattach from the same device in the same login session within `terminal.quick_reattach_window` without a new step-up; another device always needs a fresh one. | Dead zones should not train people to confirm blindly; the exception is tied to the device key. | [24 6.2](spec/24-terminal.md#62-detach-and-reattach), [R-TM-07](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T30 | N-T-06 | A host counts as the server host when the owner declares it (`hosts.declared_server_host`) or the host service reports it. | There is no reliable automatic detection; the declaration upholds SR10 even without detection. | [24 2.3](spec/24-terminal.md#23-server-host), [07 SR10](spec/07-permissions.md#sr10-terminal-only-on-own-host) |
| D-T31 | N-T-07 | Pairing requires typing the first `terminal.fingerprint_confirm_chars` characters of the fingerprint; failed attempts are limited. | As for devices ([R-ID-08](spec/08-identity.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter)): a blindly confirmed host would be a path for an intercepted code. | [24 3.2](spec/24-terminal.md#32-pairing) |
| D-T32 | N-T-08 | Pairing code in the token format of [ADR 0061](adr/0061-token-format-with-a-fixed-prefix-and-checksum.md) with its own kind; the gitleaks rule detects it. | Secret scanners should find this code too. | [ADR 0061](adr/0061-token-format-with-a-fixed-prefix-and-checksum.md), [24 3.2](spec/24-terminal.md#32-pairing) |
| D-T33 | N-T-09 | Separate purpose `terminal` of the server signing keys for terminal session tokens, kept apart from `invocation`. | An invocation token must never count as a terminal session token. | [03 `server_signing_keys`](spec/03-data-model.md#server_signing_keys), [24 5.3](spec/24-terminal.md#53-terminal-session-token) |
| D-T34 | N-T-10 | Two new rate-limit classes `rl.terminal` (per person) and `rl.host` (per host). | Distinct abuse patterns; no existing class fits. | [05 3](spec/05-actions.md#3-rate-limit-classes) |
| D-T35 | N-T-11 | Separate close codes for the three terminal channels; gateway codes apply there only where the channel names them. | The reaction of the client differs from the gateway. | [04 6.4](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#64-close-codes), [asyncapi.yaml](api/asyncapi.yaml) |
| D-T36 | N-T-12 | New problem codes `terminal_denied` (with a reason), `i6_violation`, `host_unavailable`, `fingerprint_mismatch`. | Same pattern as `agent_invocation_denied` with a reason. | [06 5](spec/06-api.md#5-errors), [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) |
| D-T37 | N-T-13 | Operations that return secrets or one-time tokens are exempt from `Idempotency-Key` (`x-idempotency-exempt: auth_flow`). | They must not be kept in the idempotency store; a retry deliberately creates new ones. | [R-API-24](spec/06-api.md#19-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-API-26](spec/06-api.md#19-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T38 | N-T-14 | Release component `host` with its own tags; the host service does not update itself; a version that is too old is rejected. | Separate release channel; a self-update would need write permissions of the service user on its binary. | [18 9.1](spec/18-release.md#91-host-service-and-server), [components.py](../tools/ci/components.py) |
| D-T39 | N-T-15 | The host service measures idle time; in addition, the server ends the session after `terminal.idle_timeout` plus `terminal.idle_grace` without bytes from the device. | A tampered host service must not keep sessions open. | [24 6.3](spec/24-terminal.md#63-limits), [R-TM-11](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-T40 | N-T-16 | The I6 check is 03 section 6.8, the tables are 4.14; guard rules SR10 and SR11 are in 07. | No renumbering of existing, widely referenced sections. | [03 6.8](spec/03-data-model.md#68-i6-check), [03 4.14](spec/03-data-model.md#414-terminal-terminal) |
| D-T41 | N-T-17 | Terminal metrics in the signal catalogue; terminal events do not go to webhooks. | Without an entry in the catalogue the code must not send the metric; terminal states are irrelevant for apps. | [signals.md 4.3a](observability/signals.md#43a-terminal) |
| D-T42 | N-T-18 | Maintenance mode blocks all terminal actions except `terminal.read_own`. | Fail closed; an exception would need a change to [ADR 0065](adr/0065-maintenance-mode-read-only.md). | [05 2.7](spec/05-actions.md#27-effect-effect) |

### 7.4 Visualization

| ID | Writer ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-V01 | VZ-R1 | `stats.live` is an ephemeral event on the existing gateway with the ops `stats.subscribe` and `stats.unsubscribe` (action `stats.read`); no separate WebSocket channel, no new action. | The gateway already brings authentication, permission checks and close codes. | [25 5](spec/25-visualization.md#5-live-stream-statslive), [R-SY-26](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#17-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V02 | VZ-R2 | The web console fetches the live frame via REST (`getStatsLive`, every `viz.live_poll_interval`). | The console does not depend on `sync_engine` ([ADR 0028](adr/0028-web-target-only-for-the-admin-and-statistics-console.md)); one schema for both paths. | [25 5.1](spec/25-visualization.md#51-flow), [R-VZ-06](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V03 | VZ-R3 | New table `stats_minutes` in the module `stats` with additive upsert and daily partitions; separate retention class `stats`, never shorter than the SLO window. | Ruling D-T7; addition needs no coordinator across nodes. | [03 4.15](spec/03-data-model.md#415-statistics-stats), [R-DM-38](spec/03-data-model.md#8-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-DP-08](spec/20-privacy.md#11-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V04 | VZ-R4 | New cross-cutting port `kernel/measure`, implemented by `stats`; the measuring modules report twice (OTel and own measurement). | A kernel port avoids cycles because `stats` sits above the measuring modules. | [02 4.5](spec/02-architecture.md#45-cross-cutting-ports-internalkernel), [R-VZ-02](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V05 | VZ-R5 | Closed metric list with exactly one `sli.*` metric per SLO; `tools/observability/sli_bounds.py` generates thresholds and targets for Go and Dart. | One source for Prometheus and the own measurement. | [25 3.2](spec/25-visualization.md#32-metrics), [25 3.3](spec/25-visualization.md#33-bounds-from-the-sloth-files), [R-VZ-03](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V06 | VZ-R6 | Measurement fails open with counted loss; the traffic light is called "own measurement" and does not alert. | Statistics never slow down the message path; alerts stay in Prometheus. | [25 3.4](spec/25-visualization.md#34-writing), [R-VZ-04](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V07 | VZ-R7 | No counters about people and no triggering people in the live frame, timeline and `stats_minutes`; the live stream can be switched off (`stats.live_enabled`). | In channels with a single person an inference would otherwise remain possible. | [25 4](spec/25-visualization.md#4-permissions-and-privacy), [R-VZ-07](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V08 | VZ-R8 | New optional field `rate_limit` (state and `resets_at`) in the heartbeat of the adapter. | The backend view per D-T8 needs the rate-limit state, without quotas or subscription names. | [09 4.8](spec/09-agents.md#48-heartbeat-and-reported-state) |
| D-V09 | VZ-R9 | In the product only a product-neutral backend view. | The product knows no credentials (I5); data about individual subscription seats does not belong in the product (D-T8). | [25 6.2](spec/25-visualization.md#62-backend-view), [R-VZ-08](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V10 | VZ-R10 | No kiosk token; TV mode runs in the session of a person, costs hidden by default (`viz.tv_show_costs`). | A token without a person and a device would be access without step-up. | [25 6.6](spec/25-visualization.md#66-tv-mode), [R-VZ-09](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V11 | VZ-R11 | `bothy_viz` draws with its own primitives (`CustomPainter`) instead of a chart library; every view has a table view. | No library has these custom shapes; accessibility. | [25 7.1](spec/25-visualization.md#71-structure), [R-VZ-10](spec/25-visualization.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V12 | VZ-R12 | Grafana plugin in the directory `plugins/grafana-bothy-live` with the ID `bothy-live-panel` and its own release component; unsigned until V1 and allowed only with this ID; queries as a generated file. | Ruling D-T11; the ID follows the scheme of create-plugin. | [25 8](spec/25-visualization.md#8-level-b-grafana-panel-plugin), [dashboards.md 5](observability/dashboards.md#5-panel-plugin-bothy-live) |
| D-V13 | VZ-R13 | Embedding in the console only with an auth proxy, `frame-ancestors`, `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy` on Grafana responses; iframe with `sandbox`. | The console is cross-origin isolated; `frame-ancestors` replaces the disabled clickjacking protection. | [25 9.2](spec/25-visualization.md#92-web-console-optional-off-by-default), [R-SE-13](spec/19-security.md#15-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-V14 | VZ-R14 | New token roles for visualizations and a TypeScript output of the tokens for the plugin; a build ticket changes the token files. | Tokens are generated, not copied ([ADR 0030](adr/0030-design-tokens-as-one-source-with-a-generator.md)). | [25 10](spec/25-visualization.md#10-shared-design-language), [components.md 11](design/components.md#11-visualizations-bothy_viz) |
| D-V15 | VZ-R15 | `bothy_viz` with fixtures and goldens as a parallel track against a mock server; server parts and views in M6, plugin in M7, signing after V1. | Owner requirement M6 and M7 with a parallel track. | [25 13](spec/25-visualization.md#13-milestones) |
| D-V16 | VZ-R16 | The generated blocks in 06 were extended by hand with the new operations. | The generator only comes in M1; without this addition the tables would be outdated at once. | [06 16](spec/06-api.md#16-endpoints), [06 17](spec/06-api.md#17-websocket-ops-and-events) |

### 7.5 Integration into chapters, plan and briefs

| ID | Writer ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-N01 | N3-01 | Product description "command center for AI agents in chat form" with six pillars; agents Hermes Agent, Claude Code and others. | Owner wording; determines the home page and the showcase. | [R-PR-13](spec/01-product.md#13-decisions-of-this-chapter), [roadmap.md](roadmap.md) |

| D-N03 | N3-03 | Key pinning before opening, against `getHost`; a mismatch only after confirmation, with `pin_changed = true`; pins in a separate local table, in the console until logout. | The audit of the opening needs the confirmation; after opening it would be too late. | [R-CL-15](spec/15-client-architecture.md#21-decisions-of-this-chapter), [R-CL-16](spec/15-client-architecture.md#21-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-N04 | N3-04 | Stream authentication of the web console via the cookie session of the WebSocket upgrade with an origin check; tokens only for native apps. | The console holds no tokens in the browser (BFF). | [15 23.1](spec/15-client-architecture.md#231-packages-and-ports), [threat-model.md 6.7](security/threat-model.md#67-terminal) |
| D-N05 | N3-05 | Terminal block in M2 (M2-38 to M2-48), views in M4, M5 and M6, release of the host service in M7, terminal in the dev environment in V2; terminal red team at the end of M2, M4, M5, M6. | Owner: the terminal is core from M2; clients build against a checked contract. | [PLAN M2-38](PLAN.md#block-terminal), [PLAN P-20](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-N06 | N3-06 | The multi-node relay of the terminal comes in M2, independent of the rest of the multi-node operation. | Ruling D-T6; otherwise the contract for multiple nodes would remain unchecked. | [PLAN M2-47](PLAN.md#block-terminal), [PLAN P-25](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-N07 | N3-07 | Visualization track M6-10 to M6-12 after the early M4 tickets; server measurement, statistics API and views M6-13 to M6-17; plugin M7-24 to M7-26; signing in V3. | Owner requirement of a parallel track; order by the dependencies (P-19). | [PLAN M6-10](PLAN.md#block-visualization), [PLAN P-21](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-N08 | N3-08 | Showcase as M7-27 (demo instance with made-up data) and M7-28 (images, video, README, docs home page, release template) instead of as a V1 ticket. | Must be finished before V1; M7 is a prerequisite of V1. | [PLAN M7-27](PLAN.md#13-m7-operations-privacy-release-and-showcase), [PLAN P-22](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan), [PLAN P-26](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-N09 | N3-09 | M1 briefs M1-02, M1-03, M1-25, M1-27, M1-28 and M1-64 minimally aligned with the addendum entries of the core contract; terminal actions stay deferred until M2. | Without the alignment these tickets would have ended `BLOCKED` (P-11). | [PLAN P-24](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan), [Brief M1-27](briefs/M1-27-authz-grant-store.md) |
| D-N10 | N3-10 | 02 section 6 is the only list of the places with the working name, grouped by kind; protocol identifiers change only with a new protocol version; the plugin ID stays `bothy-live-panel`. | Grafana requires the ID to match the account; a name change must not silently break the terminal protocol. | [02 6](spec/02-architecture.md#6-code-layout), [ADR 0044](adr/0044-working-name-and-branding-as-configuration.md) |

## 8. Rulings of the gap integration

Before M1, all items from three lists of spec gaps were decided (contradictions between
chapters, briefs and API documents). The results are not copied here but stand where they
apply: as an R-ID in the section "Decisions of this chapter", as text in the chapter, in the
API documents, as `P-<n>` or `W<n>` in the plan or in the briefs.

### 8.2 Rulings outside the chapters

These decisions of the gap integration stand in no chapter, because they concern the build order,
tools or operations docs. The number is that of the gap item.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-G-33 | The project's own check scripts are listed in `gates.md`, pinned third-party tools in `toolchain.md`. | The test of `toolchain.md` holds the versions against the pins. | [tools/README.md](../tools/README.md), [gates.md](quality/gates.md) |
| D-G-39 | M1 rejects violations of I1 with `409 i1_violation`; the guided flow `resolve_i1` comes additively with M2-21. | No contradiction between milestones; the field is additive. | [PLAN M2-21](PLAN.md#block-agents-and-extensions) |
| D-G-55 | One list of reason codes in `kernel/auditing.Reason`; `authorize.Reason` without constants of its own. | Kernel ports must not import each other (02 7.2). | [Brief M1-28](briefs/M1-28-authz-core.md) |
| D-G-96 | Metric `bothy.secrets.refresh.failures` with an alert and a runbook. | Otherwise a silently failing secret refresh is only noticed at expiry. | [signals.md](observability/signals.md#46-backup-audit-telemetry) |
| D-G-99 | The GenAI conventions are encapsulated in `internal/platform/otel/genai`; the state of the convention is marked as open, not invented. | Cannot be determined without network access. | [signals.md 6](observability/signals.md#6-genai-conventions-encapsulated) |
| D-G-113 | No structured field per action in `actions.yaml`; a source code test per action keeps them in agreement. | A later extension of [ADR 0049](adr/0049-machine-readable-action-catalogue-as-the-single-source.md) without a need in M1. | [Brief M1-28](briefs/M1-28-authz-core.md), [ADR 0049](adr/0049-machine-readable-action-catalogue-as-the-single-source.md) |
| D-G-118 | One counter `bothy.authz.errors` with a reason for `unknown_action` and `authz_not_checked`, with an alert. | Internal errors of the authorization must be visible without going out as a problem code. | [signals.md](observability/signals.md#41-rest-and-gateway) |
| D-G-124 | `CONTRIBUTING.md` is corrected with the first Go module in M1-01. | Today the file is correct because there is no Go code. | [Brief M1-01](briefs/M1-01-server-gates.md) |
| D-G-125 | `quality.perf.timeout` is added to 23 with M1-01 and its watchdog test. | Entered now, the test would fail without a Makefile target. | [Brief M1-01](briefs/M1-01-server-gates.md) |
| D-G-126 | The action catalogue is reconciled once against a frozen copy. | A permanent test on live data would not be reproducible. | [PLAN M1-02](PLAN.md#block-a-toolchain) |
| D-G-127 | System actions are in no list of `redocly.yaml`. | System actions have no route. | [redocly.yaml](api/redocly.yaml) |
| D-G-136 | The PromQL of the dashboards is checked as a temporary rule file with the pinned `promtool`. | M1-68 reads all dashboard expressions anyway; no new wave. | [PLAN M1-68](PLAN.md#block-h-closing-the-milestone) |
| D-G-153 | Personal API tokens are off by default until M1-47; M1-47 registers the switch. | A switch needs the flag registration. | [PLAN M1-47](PLAN.md#block-e-instance-and-management-of-permissions) |
| D-G-259 | Until they are enabled, fields of later milestones answer with `400 validation_failed` with `errors[].code` `not_enabled`; every plan row names the field that it enables. | A client recognizes the state unambiguously. | [Brief M1-56](briefs/M1-56-messaging-send.md) |
| D-G-280 | Until M2-05 and M2-20, `active_runs` in the snapshot is empty and agents have `state: unknown`. | M1-63 wires the port empty, because the table `runs` only exists from M2-05 on. | [Brief M1-63](briefs/M1-63-sync-catch-up.md), [PLAN M2-05](PLAN.md#block-agents-and-extensions) |
| D-G-301 | P-04 also applies to gateway ops (section `gateway_ops` with `enabled` and `later`). | One mechanism for REST and gateway. | [PLAN P-04](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-G-304 | `bothy.event.fanout.lag` is the age of the oldest event not yet distributed, `0` when idle. | This way the alert fits without a change. | [signals.md 4.2](observability/signals.md#42-events-and-delivery) |
| D-G-305 | Errors of the clients at the gateway count as `client_error` and do not consume the error budget. | The budget measures the server. | [signals.md 4.1](observability/signals.md#41-rest-and-gateway) |
| D-G-310 | Instruments of libraries run through an SDK view with an allow-list of attributes. | Otherwise, for example, `server.address` from the `Host` header would get into the metrics. | [signals.md 2](observability/signals.md#2-cardinality) |
| D-G-330 | Test overlay with the `file` exporter only for tests, not in the release bundle, checked with `otelcol-contrib validate`. | The canary test needs a readable output. | [privacy.md](observability/privacy.md#3-pseudonyms) |
| D-G-331 | The installation Compose stays unchanged until M7; the smoke overlay leaves out keys of later milestones. | An installation must not fail on keys that the schema of the milestone does not know. | [PLAN P-06](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan) |
| D-G-346 | Before the history scan, every identity that is not the one chosen by the owner is removed from the allow-list of authors (D-W-06). | Only then does a scan without a finding prove that no other identity is in the history. | [public-switch.md A](quality/public-switch.md#a-before-the-push) |
| D-G-347 | The four closing documents are created in M0-04; until then only the references to them remain open. | Exit criteria of M0 without a chicken-and-egg problem. | [PLAN M0-04](PLAN.md#6-m0-planning-closing) |
| D-G-DEP | Dependencies of the M1 tickets are minimal (direct only); waves are computed from them. | Transitive dependencies in every row would go stale with every change. | [briefs/README.md](briefs/README.md#waves) |

## 9. Ruling log of the resumption

The third pass of the gap integration aborted without writing a log; its changes lay uncommitted
in the working tree. Planning was then resumed.

| ID | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-W-01 | The resumption checked every item of the aborted pass against the state in the working tree (done, partial, open, to be rejected, with the location of the evidence) and completed the items that were not complete. No item counts as done just because a file was touched. | An aborted writer leaves changes without proof; only the check per item proves the state. | Commits from "docs(kern): core contract extended after gaps part 3" to "docs(briefs): PLAN and M1 briefs aligned after gaps part 3" |
| D-W-02 | Decisions of the first two passes are not reverted. | The briefs build on them; reverting them would be a new decision without a new reason. | Section 8 |
| D-W-03 | The owner addendum (terminal, visualization, showcase) was added in the same resumption: design with the rulings T1 to T11, then core contract, chapters 24 and 25, ADRs, plan and briefs. | The planning state before the resumption contained neither; the owner requirement is binding. | Section 7, [PLAN 2](PLAN.md#2-milestone-breakdown) |
| D-W-04 | One writer in the main tree at a time; reviewers only read; every finding of a reviewer is fixed and seen by the next fresh reviewer. | No overlapping changes; no finding counts as fixed without a fresh look. | [PLAN 4](PLAN.md#4-closing-a-milestone) |
| D-W-05 | The closing documents (SPEC, DECISIONS, OPEN-QUESTIONS, RISKS, README, navigation, CHANGELOG) are written last; until then their absence is the only expected warning in the strict docs build. | They summarize the state and can only be correct afterwards. | [PLAN M0-04](PLAN.md#6-m0-planning-closing), D-G-347 |
| D-W-06 | Commit identity: the owner chose the identity of the commits; it counts as a public identity in the sense of `public-switch.md` step 2. The private deny list allows it only in the author and committer fields; every other occurrence of the same domain stays forbidden. Rewriting the authors is dropped. Before the first push, only the rewrite with `git filter-repo --replace-text` for the old sample values of the gitleaks exception remains; afterwards the exception is deleted (M0-08). Current state: D-W-36 and D-W-48 (M0-08 also removes every hit of the history scan, with its own tool per location). Identities in `allow-authors.txt` that are not the chosen one are removed before the history scan. The product repo names neither the name nor the address. Current state: D-W-93 (what is published is a root commit without parents; the rewrite before the first push is dropped). | Owner decision (D-L18); the white label in the content is preserved. | [public-switch.md A](quality/public-switch.md#a-before-the-push) |
| D-W-07 | Design accepted on the preview images: dark petrol, yellow accent, working name Bothy. | Owner acceptance. | [design/previews](design/previews/layout-dark.png), D-DS-01 |
| D-W-08 | Ticket numbers are only names. The build order is set by the dependencies, for M1 by the waves in [briefs/README.md](briefs/README.md#waves); dependencies never point to a later milestone and never form a cycle ([PLAN P-19](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan), reworded). Existing dependencies on higher numbers of the same milestone stay. | The earlier version of P-19 required dependencies only on lower numbers; some tickets violated that. Renumbering would break the names of the briefs and many references without changing the order, which the waves already show completely. | [PLAN P-19](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan), [briefs/README.md](briefs/README.md#waves) |
| D-W-09 | New doctor check HD9: the session user of the host service must not be able to list any home folder of another user; checked as a read test, not via groups. Current state: HD9 checks only home folders of users that are not session users; the home of a session user is checked by HD11 (D-W-88). | On macOS the default group `staff` opens other users' home folders; the read test works the same on every platform. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host), [R-TM-17](spec/24-terminal.md#16-decisions-of-this-chapter), TM-T24 |
| D-W-10 | The integrations from requirement 19 are build tickets: reference app code hosting (M2-49, TypeScript SDK), reference app ticket system (M2-50, Python SDK), cron path with a test in M2-27, alerts in M7-20. Tests use only fake APIs with fixtures from the vendors' documentation. | Finding L-01 of the critic: 11 only announced the apps, no ticket built them; the requirement asks for them explicitly. Real services in tests would be external accounts. | [11 10](spec/11-extensions.md#10-ready-made-integrations-as-examples), [R-EX-11](spec/11-extensions.md#14-decisions-of-this-chapter), [PLAN 8](PLAN.md#8-m2-agent-platform-extensions-and-terminal) |
| D-W-11 | Installation in one command via the setup wizard `install.sh` (POSIX shell on the host, release artifact with built-in version and checksum of the bundle), ticket M7-29; the portability test installs with exactly its options (source of the script by phase, D-W-59). No wizard in the container; no random secrets in `.env`, `secrets-init` still generates them; off-site stays optional with a warning. | Finding L-02: requirement 21 and the addendum require one command and a setup wizard; no ticket built it. A container would need the Docker socket (root on the host); secrets in `.env` would contradict R-OP-05. | [16 3.1](spec/16-operations.md#31-installation-in-one-command-setup-wizard), [R-OP-20](spec/16-operations.md#15-decisions-of-this-chapter), [Installation](operations/install-compose.md) |
| D-W-12 | Entry screens E1 to E7 (server address, login, enrollment code, device waiting, pairing, app lock, logged out) shared by all apps; the console uses E2 and E7. | Finding L-19: M4-18 built these flows without screens. | [01 5.6](spec/01-product.md#56-onboarding-and-sign-in-all-apps), [R-PR-14](spec/01-product.md#13-decisions-of-this-chapter) |
| D-W-13 | Terminal view as a component of the design system with its own roles `color.terminal.*` (surface, text, cursor, selection, 16 ANSI colors from the existing palette); the roles `color.viz.*` go into the tokens already now, M6-10 adds the check for color vision deficiency. New preview images terminal and statistics; the accepted images stay unchanged (`tokens.py previews --view`). Current state: D-W-46 (D-W-41 afterwards regenerated seven accepted images) and D-W-77 (the new images fall under the acceptance). The three grays ANSI 0, 7 and 15 as well as the cursor need contrast `ui`, all other terminal colors `text`. | Finding L-20: the terminal was missing from the design system, `color.viz.*` stood in components.md without a token. Only an addition: the accepted design (D-W-07) does not change. Full text contrast for all three grays cannot be reached in both themes at once. | [components.md 12](design/components.md#12-terminal-view), [contrast.md](design/contrast.md), [PLAN M6-10](PLAN.md#12-m6-admin-console-statistics-and-visualization) |
| D-W-14 | Docs home page with a showcase standard (image light and dark, six pillars, quick start, reading path per role); links to repository files are relative, the MkDocs hook builds the URL from `repo_url`. New gate in `make linkcheck`: no link outside code to the placeholder repository `OWNER`. | Finding L-22: the home page had no image, no quick start and a dead link; lychee checks offline and did not see it. | [Home page](index.md), [gates.md](quality/gates.md), `tools/docs/placeholder_links.py` |
| D-W-15 | One contract for expired and replaced invocation tokens: the adapter acknowledges after the acceptance into the inbox, not after `run.start`; a retry with a new token replaces the stored one; a token error at start is always `403 invocation_token_invalid` with `reason` `expired`, `superseded` or `invalid`; an expired token that is still current is renewed by the new action `run.token.renew` (`POST /runs/{id}/invocation-token`), which repeats all checks before a delivery. The earlier codes `invocation_token_expired` (401) and `stale_invocation_token` are dropped. | Finding L-07: three incompatible contracts; a start longer than `agent.ack_timeout` triggered a redelivery whose token replaced the old one and which the duplicate detection discarded, and the run hung until `run.queued_max_age`. The renewed token is not a new authorization, because the server decides anew. | [09 4.3](spec/09-agents.md#43-connection-and-cursor), [09 4.5](spec/09-agents.md#45-invocation-token), [04 15.2](spec/04-sync-protocol.md#152-ack-and-redelivery), [06](spec/06-api.md), AG-28, AG-29 |
| D-W-16 | Tool class in the protocol chapter: claims `tool_class` and `dev_environment_id` in 09, check point "reported class not higher than configured class" in the adapter, required field `tool_class_used` in `startRun` and `resumeRun` (server: `409 tool_class_mismatch`), reasons `tool_class_mismatch`, `tool_class_owner_only` and `channel_read_only` in 09 5.2; the runner maps `chat`, `scoped`, `host` to `--permission-mode` and `--tools`, `host` is `bypassPermissions`. | Finding L-08: 06 promised a `409` at start without a field in the body; unknown reasons would have been stored as `adapter_error`; the class `full` did not exist. | [09 4.5](spec/09-agents.md#45-invocation-token), [09 18.2](spec/09-agents.md#182-process-contract), AG-30 |
| D-W-17 | With `llm_credentials = personal`, `bothy-host` does not touch any subscription login: no token in the state folder, no reading, no handover. The holder of the login logs in themselves in the account of the session user (`/login` or their own token from `claude setup-token` in a file of the session user); the profile `claude` then starts via that user's login shell. Ruling T10 applies more narrowly: the long-lived token remains the path for a user without a login session, but it lies with the session user. | Finding L-03: the earlier version put the token in the state folder and passed it into the PTY; the product would have stored and brokered a Claude.ai login, against 10 consequence 3 and I5. | [24 7.4](spec/24-terminal.md#74-enforcement-on-the-host), [OQ-03](OPEN-QUESTIONS.md#oq-03-long-lived-subscription-token-in-the-terminal), TM-T25 |
| D-W-18 | The PTY helper is a system service under the session user that the session user cannot change, stop or replace: binary, service definition and channel endpoint owned by `root` or `Administrators`, respectively, endpoint from the service manager, verification of the peer by user and process ID, memory protection; new check HD10, HD8 additionally by the service user. Owners see a warning before opening on the host of another person; the audit names `foreign_assignee`. New threat T-TM-12. | Finding L-04: a helper owned by the session user could be replaced; check results could be forged and later sessions could be read along. | D-W-80 | HD7, HD10 and HD11 use fixed terms per platform (24 2.2 item 6). Trusted owners: Linux and macOS `root`, Windows `SYSTEM`, `Administrators` and `NT SERVICE\TrustedInstaller`; every other account does not count, not even that of a person with administrator privileges. "Writable" means, for files, changing or deleting content, attributes, permissions or ownership, for folders additionally creating, deleting or renaming entries: under Linux and macOS `w` from the mode, under Linux also from the ACL; under macOS in addition the ACL permissions `write` and `append` (files), `add_file`, `add_subdirectory`, `delete_child` (folders), `delete`, `writeattr`, `writeextattr`, `writesecurity`, `chown`; under Windows `FILE_ADD_FILE`, `FILE_ADD_SUBDIRECTORY`, `FILE_DELETE_CHILD`, `FILE_WRITE_DATA`, `FILE_APPEND_DATA`, `FILE_WRITE_ATTRIBUTES`, `FILE_WRITE_EA`, `DELETE`, `WRITE_DAC`, `WRITE_OWNER` for the session user or a group in its token; entries only for inheritance do not count for the folder itself. Current state: permissions for files and attributes (D-W-86). Under Windows, `bothy-host install`, after confirmation by the instance operator, removes the default entry `Authenticated Users:(AD)` at the root of the system drive and of every drive with a location; `install --drive-root <drive>` performs this step for a drive added later. Without confirmation HD11 reports `drive_root_writable` and `doctor` refuses the start. The command leaves the inheritable entry `Authenticated Users:(OI)(CI)(IO)(M)` in place. Current state: The command writes the DACL of the root without propagation to the folders below (D-W-87). Since then, the terms "`root` or `Administrators`" in older rows of this page mean a trusted owner. | On a Windows host with default permissions HD11 never passed, because `C:\` allows all logged-in users to create folders and the system folders belong to `TrustedInstaller`; if "writable" was read without creating, a person could lock out every other person with `C:\.git`, the pattern of the gap in Git for Windows (sources in 24 2.2). The chosen option is removing only `(AD)`: It applies to the root alone and changes nothing below. Windows would propagate the removal of the inheritable entry to every existing folder below and take write permissions from programs; the folders that inherit it are found by HD11 when they lie on the path to a location. | D-W-89 | Consequences of HD11 during operation: A violation at a location for all ends all running sessions with `host_policy_violation` and `close_detail = shared_location` and allows no new ones until HD11 passes; the message names no session user. The check of the entries runs per session user in its PTY helper; at start, only this session user waits for its run (code `entry_scan_running`), on command the previous result applies until the end. `host_service.entry_scan_timeout` limits a run; after that, HD11 fails for this session user (`entry_scan_timeout`). A finding of this check, including an abort, is lifted only by a later full run (start or `bothy-host doctor`), never by a periodic one. `bothy-host doctor` performs the full run in a running host service; if it finds a violation with "Start refused", except for HD6, HD10 and HD11, the host service exits as with a refused start. Current state: with `entry_scan_timeout` the person cannot reach their home without a session; the instance operator raises the value or makes the home smaller themselves ([16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service) item 6). | HD11 runs before every PTY and periodically, but for a location for all only "Start refused" was stated; HD10 governed operation, HD11 did not. The check of the entries runs only at start and on command, but the block applied "until HD11 passes", and whether a periodic run lifts it was open; a periodic run does not see the entry and therefore must not declare it settled. Its duration depends on the size of the home; without a limit, the whole host would wait for the largest home. An aborted run has not seen every entry and therefore does not count as passed; the consequence affects only its session user. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host) HD11 and "check of the entries", [24 17](spec/24-terminal.md#17-configuration-values-of-this-chapter) `host_service.entry_scan_timeout`, R-TM-28, [16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service) item 6, [RISKS RK-90](RISKS.md), TM-T29 (e) and (f), PLAN M2-42, M2-45 |
| D-W-90 | `bothy-host session-user add` restarts the host service like `remove`; afterwards the host reports the name with the next `report`, and the owner assigns it without a further step. Every other change to the configuration of the host service (working directories, profiles, `host_service.claude_bin`) takes effect after `bothy-host restart`. Every restart of the host service ends all sessions of the host: Every PTY helper ends its PTYs as soon as its channel to the host service breaks off; on the control connection, `identify` names the sessions that the host service still knows (`live_sessions`), and the server ends every other session of the host in `active` or `detached` with the new `close_reason` `host_restarted`. | The host service reads its configuration only at start. After `add`, the server rejected the new name with `session_user_not_on_host` until someone restarted by hand, and for working directories there was no way. What a restart does with running sessions of other people was open, and `TerminalCloseReason` had no matching value. Letting the PTYs keep running was rejected: The new process checks everything again at start and reads the configuration again; a session from the time before would keep running unchecked under the old configuration, for example in a removed working directory, and its scrollback buffer lay in the old process. The list in `identify` needs no state in the server and distinguishes a restart from a network drop. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host) items 3 and 5, [24 6.2](spec/24-terminal.md#62-detach-and-reattach), [24 10.3](spec/24-terminal.md#103-reasons-for-ending-terminal_sessionsclose_reason), R-TM-27, [16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service), [03 `terminal_sessions`](spec/03-data-model.md), [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) `HostIdentify`, `TerminalCloseReason`, [asyncapi.yaml](api/asyncapi.yaml) `hostControl`, [ADR 0068](adr/0068-terminal-through-a-host-service-with-noise-stream-and-invariant-i6.md), TM-T16, TM-T21 (f), PLAN M2-42, M2-43, M2-45 |

If `doctor` refuses the start at start, the host service starts no PTY and accepts no session, but reports first if it is paired: login, one `report`, then it closes the connection and exits with its own exit code, after which the service manager does not restart it by itself; after the fix, the instance operator starts it with `bothy-host restart`. Ruling T2 stays: On a contradiction with the declaration there is no session. | The promise that the console names every affected session user applied only to a hit during operation. If HD6 triggered at start, for example after a restart of the host or after `session-user remove`, there was no `report`; the console showed the host only as disconnected, with the result from the time before, and what `doctor` prints on the host was not defined. A restart by the service manager fixes none of these violations and only logged the host in again and again in a loop. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host) "Output on the host" and "Message on a refused start", [24 7.4](spec/24-terminal.md#74-enforcement-on-the-host), R-TM-29, [16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service) items 2, 3 and 6, [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) `HostReport`, [asyncapi.yaml](api/asyncapi.yaml) `hostControl`, [Threat model T-TM-14](security/threat-model.md), [RISKS RK-89](RISKS.md), TM-T21 (g), PLAN M2-42, M2-43 |

| D-W-109 | Rule for names in `hosts.reported_doctor`: If a person with terminal access, also a former one, can cause a violation, the message to the server names the account in which the instance operator must look, each account with its own entry (`session_user`); a new check follows the same rule. These are HD6 and HD11 (as before), new: HD10 with a wrong peer (the session user of this channel) and HD9 for every former session user (name bound to a person in the state directory, no longer in `host_service.session_users`) whose home can be listed; the console also shows the person of its assignment. Names of other users of the host are named only by the output on the host; for them an entry without a name goes to the server. HD9 stays at "Start refused". After `bothy-host session-user remove` the instance operator removes the long-running processes of the user, ends its processes and deletes it together with its home (or first moves the home to a location only for administrators); locking alone is not enough. After the access removal of a person who is not an owner, the console advises this step. | `remove` closes the home but ends no processes. A long-running process that the person set up as the session user reopens the home, because it still belongs to their user; HD9 then refused the start of the whole host, and the console got only the code. The same applied to HD10 when a process of a person takes the place of their helper. This was the same error as with HD6 before D-W-64: a person brings the host to a standstill, and the console does not name them. Instead of extending every check one by one, one rule applies to every check that a person can make fail; the server knows these names from the assignment and needs no other names. Locking ends no running process, and `passwd -l` under Linux locks only the password ("Note that this does not disable the account", https://manpages.debian.org/bookworm/passwd/passwd.1.en.html, retrieved 2026-10-11 [verified]). Only deleting takes the home out of HD9 and ends the path; otherwise a later user with the same user ID would get the home without a user. Limit: after the loss of the state directory, the name of a former session user is missing from the message. | [24 2.2](spec/24-terminal.md#22-host-service-bothy-host) items 3 and 4, HD9, HD10, "Reason for HD9" and rule for names, [24 6.5](spec/24-terminal.md#65-end-of-a-session-on-the-host), [24 10.1](spec/24-terminal.md#101-audit), R-TM-30, [16 4.4](spec/16-operations.md#44-terminal-forwarding-and-host-service) items 1 and 6, [03 `hosts`](spec/03-data-model.md), [openapi.yaml](api/openapi.yaml) `HostDoctorCheck`, [01 A8](spec/01-product.md), [20](spec/20-privacy.md), [ADR 0068](adr/0068-terminal-through-a-host-service-with-noise-stream-and-invariant-i6.md), [Threat model T-TM-04, T-TM-11, T-TM-12, T-TM-14](security/threat-model.md), [RISKS RK-26, RK-89](RISKS.md), TM-T24, TM-T26, PLAN M2-42, M4-37 |
| D-W-110 | `check_runners.py` recognizes release jobs independently of the spelling: it compares environment names without case, and blanks at the edges do not count. New rule `environment-not-literal`: an environment name with `${{` (as text or in `environment.name`) is a finding in every workflow. Not covered is where the secrets lie in GitHub: signing secrets exist only as secrets of the environments `release*` (ADR 0039, rule 4). The planning branch also compared runner labels without case and required `bothy-ci` or `bothy-release` in every self-hosted switch; on `main` there are no self-hosted runners (D-W-92), and a label in another spelling is already a finding of `runner-not-github-hosted`. | GitHub treats environment names without case ("Environment names are not case sensitive", https://docs.github.com/en/actions/how-tos/deploy/configure-and-manage-deployments/manage-environments, retrieved 2026-10-11) [verified]. With the literal comparison, a job with `environment: Release` got the signing secrets and passed outputs of `resolve` to a shell, without a finding. GitHub allows an expression as the name ("The value of `name` can be an expression", https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax, section `jobs.<job_id>.environment`, retrieved 2026-10-11) [verified]; whether it yields an environment `release*` would only be known at run time, and no workflow file needs it. These rules had already missed two forms (D-W-61, D-W-72); so the check now compares like GitHub itself instead of adding single spellings. | `tools/ci/check_runners.py`, `tools/ci/tests/test_check_runners.py`, [ADR 0039](adr/0039-only-github-hosted-runners-public-from-the-first-push.md), [gates.md](quality/gates.md), [Threat model T-SC-05](security/threat-model.md), R-QA-12, D-W-61 |
| D-W-111 | New rule `actions-write` in `tools/ci/check_runners.py`: no workflow has `actions: write`, neither in `permissions` on top level nor on a job, also not on a job with `uses`; `write-all` is now a finding on a job too (`broad-permissions`). There is no exception, also not for `release.yml`; if a workflow needs the permission later, that is a decision of its own. Not covered is a token from a secret with permission on Actions. | The rule `reusable-release-caller` (D-W-73) saw only calls by `uses:`. A pull request workflow with `actions: write` could start `portability.yml` with `gh workflow run`: events created with the `GITHUB_TOKEN` start no run otherwise, but "`workflow_dispatch` and `repository_dispatch` events always create workflow runs" (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/security/github_token, retrieved 2026-10-11) [verified], and the endpoint `POST /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/workflows/{workflow_id}/dispatches` requires the permission "Actions" with `write` for the installation token of a GitHub App, which the `GITHUB_TOKEN` is (https://docs.github.com/en/rest/authentication/permissions-required-for-github-apps, retrieved 2026-10-11) [verified]. The release workflow would then run outside the one run per tag of its release. Blocking the permission hits every path to a `workflow_dispatch` (gh, REST API, an action) in one place, instead of searching for commands in steps, and no workflow needs it. `release.yml` neither: with the permission it could start `portability.yml` a second time next to the call. A `repository_dispatch` is already allowed by `contents: write`; it starts only workflows that name this trigger (https://docs.github.com/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax, retrieved 2026-10-11) [verified], which no release workflow may name (rule `release-trigger`). On the planning branch the reason was also the minutes budget of the private phase, which `main` does not have (D-W-92). | `tools/ci/check_runners.py`, `tools/ci/tests/test_check_runners.py`, [ADR 0039](adr/0039-only-github-hosted-runners-public-from-the-first-push.md), [gates.md](quality/gates.md), [23 4](spec/23-quality.md#4-ci), D-W-73 |
| D-W-112 | Port of the planning branch to `main` (2026-10-12): the planning rounds after the public baseline come to `main` in English with their IDs, as several pull requests. Three IDs of the planning branch are taken on `main` by other rulings (D-W-92, D-W-93, D-W-94 since the public baseline and the translation); their rulings carry new IDs here: D-W-92 of the planning branch is D-W-109, D-W-93 is D-W-110, D-W-94 is D-W-111, and every mention follows. Parts that rest on the self-hosted runners and the minutes budget of the private phase (`docs/quality/self-hosted-runner.md`, `docs/quality/ci-budget.md`, rule `release-runner-label`) are not ported, because `main` has only GitHub-hosted runners (D-W-92); D-W-110 and D-W-111 say what remains. | The public baseline was built from an earlier state of the planning branch than its later rounds and reused D-W-92 and D-W-93; the translation added D-W-94. Renumbering the three later rulings keeps every ID unique (`register_ids.py`) and leaves the rulings that `main` already cites unchanged. | D-W-92, D-W-93, D-W-94, D-W-109, D-W-110, D-W-111 |

## 10. Rulings of the build milestones

From M1 on, every ticket records here what it decides and no chapter specifies: ID
`D-<Ticket>-<n>` (for example `D-M1-17-1`), date, decision, reason, location. If a ticket changes
a row above, the row stays and names the new ID.

| ID | Date | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-DEPS-1 | 2026-10-11 | The development tools pin `katex` through `overrides` in `tools/node/package.json` to `0.18.10`, the newest 0.18 release that is not deprecated (0.18.11 is deprecated by its authors: https://registry.npmjs.org/katex/0.18.11, retrieved 2026-10-11). The pin goes when mermaid and mermaid-cli accept a fixed katex themselves; Renovate proposes newer pins in the group "dev tools (Node)". | GHSA-238p-pmpm-9mq7 is fixed from 0.18.2 (https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-238p-pmpm-9mq7, retrieved 2026-10-11), but mermaid-cli 11.17.0 asks for `katex@^0.16.25` and mermaid (also the newest 12.1.0) for `^0.16.47` (https://registry.npmjs.org/@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli/11.17.0, https://registry.npmjs.org/mermaid/12.1.0, retrieved 2026-10-11), so the Dependabot security update failed with "conflicting dependencies" and no tool release resolves to a fixed version. mermaid-cli uses the installed package only for the katex style sheet; mermaid's browser bundle carries its own katex code, which the override does not change, and the repository's diagrams contain no math. `make diagrams` renders every diagram with the pin. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools), [package.json](../tools/node/package.json) |
| D-DEPS-2 | 2026-10-11 | GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv (node-forge up to 1.4.0, RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification) is dismissed on GitHub as "vulnerable code not used" and stays an accepted OSV finding with an expiry date (D-QA-19). No override and no removal: no node-forge release has the fix (https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv, retrieved 2026-10-11), and the newest `@sonar/scan` (5.0.1) pins node-forge 1.4.0 exactly (https://registry.npmjs.org/@sonar/scan/5.0.1, retrieved 2026-10-11). | `@sonar/scan` imports node-forge only in `src/request.js` (source of 5.0.1: https://github.com/SonarSource/sonar-scanner-npm/blob/bfa5f4128eb69f902e09190f7ad1a41dc65b1912/src/request.ts, default truststore in https://github.com/SonarSource/sonar-scanner-npm/blob/bfa5f4128eb69f902e09190f7ad1a41dc65b1912/src/properties.ts, retrieved 2026-10-11), to read a PKCS#12 truststore when `sonar.scanner.truststorePath` is set or `<sonar.userHome>/ssl/truststore.p12` exists; it calls `util.decode64`, `asn1.fromDer`, `pkcs12.pkcs12FromAsn1`, `getBags` and `certificateToPem`, none of which verifies an RSA signature (PKCS#12 integrity is an HMAC), and the certificates go to Node's TLS stack, which does the verification. The repository configures no truststore, and even with one the path verifies no signature. A probe on 2026-10-11 ran exactly these calls on a truststore with an RSA certificate (exponent 3) while every RSA verify entry point threw: zero calls. Replacing the scanner would need Java or Docker for the scan, which the toolchain avoids on purpose. | [osv-scanner.toml](../tools/node/osv-scanner.toml), [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools) |

### M1-01 Server gates

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-01/1 | 2026-10-10 | `<module>/migrations.sha256` holds the immutability of merged migrations; only `make migrations-lock` writes new lines, and it never changes an existing line. By hand, a branch removes only its own line that is not yet merged, when it renumbers. | ADR 0007 rule 5 and "Confirmation"; before the first push there is no diff against a remote base branch. |
| M1-01/2 | 2026-10-10 | The server gates require go, a Docker-compatible runtime and the pins as soon as a Go module is tracked, including gates without tests. | R-QA-02 and PLAN P-09: A host that cannot run the integration tests never reports a half-green server state. |
| M1-01/3 | 2026-10-10 | `codegen-check` detects drift through snapshots before and after the generator instead of through `git diff`. | Manual changes in a dirty working tree are not drift of the generator. |
| M1-01/4 | 2026-10-10 | `make perf` has the time limit `quality.perf.timeout`. | The default limit of `go test` would end long-running tests early; the value is a named threshold like all others in chapter 23. |
| M1-01/5 | 2026-10-10 | `tools/bin/tools.lock.json` is in the layout that `make tools-lock` writes (JSON with indentation 2); `tools/bin/tests/test_fetch_tools.py` holds that. | In the earlier compact layout, every re-pin rewrote the whole file, and the changed checksums got lost in the diff. |
| M1-01/6 | 2026-10-10 | `make migrations-lock` refuses on an unreadable line or a duplicate name in `migrations.sha256` and writes nothing; when it writes, it establishes the order and the line ending at the end of the file. | With an unreadable or duplicate line it would have to discard or choose a line, that is, change an existing line. |
| M1-01/7 | 2026-10-10 | `migrations-lint` skips a Go module only if neither `migrations/` nor `migrations.sha256` exists; a checksum file without a directory fails. | If `migrations/` is deleted while the checksum file stays, this is noticed. If both are deleted together, if a migration is changed by hand together with its line, or if the newest migration is removed together with its line, the gate does not see it; this limit is stated in `docs/quality/gates.md` under "What the gates do not cover". |
| M1-01/8 | 2026-10-10 | The recipes of the server gates explicitly abort with `exit 1` after every failed step instead of relying on `.SHELLFLAGS`. | GNU make 3.81 (macOS) does not know `.SHELLFLAGS` and would continue after an error. |
| M1-01/9 | 2026-10-10 | Go coverage is computed over a copy of `coverage.out` without the lines of the packages under `test/` and `perf/`; `coverage.out` itself stays unchanged. | SonarQube reads `coverage.out` in `make check` and in the CI job that only runs `make sonar`; only this way does it see the same file in both places. Until M1-72 adds `services/server/perf/**` to `sonar.test.inclusions` (brief M1-72, scope 8), SonarQube counts the files under `perf/` without `_test.go` (such as `perf/internal/report` from M1-16 on) as production code. |
| M1-01/10 | 2026-10-10 | `go_prereqs.py` reports every error while reading the lock file or while first loading a pin (network error, aborted download, broken lock file) as a problem of the gate (`<gate>: <tool>: <error>`). | An abort with a traceback does not say which tool is missing and loses the problems already collected. |
| M1-01/11 | 2026-10-10 | `tools/conftest.py` (new, outside the file list of the brief) removes, for every test, the variables with which Git hooks point to a repository (`GIT_DIR` and related ones); `codegen_check.py` does not pass them on to `git ls-files`. | The pre-push hook calls `make check`; from a linked worktree pytest inherits an absolute `GIT_DIR`, and the Git fixtures of the tests would have reconfigured the real repository and created a commit that deletes all files. The error class was already present in older Git fixtures under `tools/`; it is fixed in one place, and `tools/ci/tests/test_git_env_isolation.py` holds that. |

It is `false` for the exceptions from 04 "Delivery guarantee" item 3, all resources without a column `version` (among others `user`, `file`, `instance`, `grant`, `group`, `principal`, `auth_session`, `gdpr_request`, `import_source`, `interaction`), `draft.deleted` and the ephemeral events except `run.updated`; `run.updated` is `true`. | Rule of the core contract: 04 "Envelope" gives all events the same envelope, and "Delivery guarantee" item 3 says when `resource.version` is set; without a required field, a silent default would decide. A draft is removed when it is deleted, so there is no new value; `settings.updated`, `notification_pref.updated` and `quiet_hours.updated` have the resource `user` without a column `version` and carry the version of their sub-object in the payload, like `read_state.updated`; `run.updated` reports the new `runs.version` (04 "Live box streaming"), the other ephemeral events report no new version of their object. |
